Method: Utilizing two scalar fields in the representations of $SU(15)_p$, the authors identify flavour-breaking spurions generating Yukawa couplings and studying their correlations, employing a Froggatt-Nielsen-like texture to fit quark and lepton masses alongside flavour-changing processes.

Result: The model successfully reproduces all six quark masses, three lepton masses, and provides a benchmark fit for CKM matrix predictions; it shows that the electron EDM and $K^0$-$ar{K}^0$ mixing reach significant sensitivity at high energy scales.

Conclusion: Major flavour-changing processes and observables indicate a strong sensitivity to compositeness scale, with predictions of sensitivity extending into the $10^6$ TeV range, surpassing current experimental capabilities.

Abstract: We study the flavour structure of an $SU(15)_p$ confining chiral gauge theory in which the Standard Model (SM) quarks, leptons, and Higgs emerge as composite bound states. The couplings of two scalar fields in the conjugate antisymmetric ($\overline{\mathbf{105}}$) and conjugate symmetric ($\overline{\mathbf{120}}$) representations of $SU(15)_p$ provide two $SU(4)_F$ flavour-breaking spurions that generate both the SM Yukawa couplings and the flavour-changing processes. The up and down Yukawa matrices are tightly-correlated due to a "right-handed isospin" symmetry, which predicts a trivial CKM matrix in the absence of spontaneous symmetry breaking. The lepton Yukawas are correlated with the quarks due to a common source of flavour spurions. With a judicious Froggatt-Nielsen-like texture for the two flavour spurions we find that a benchmark fit with $\mathcal{O}(1)$ non-perturbative coefficients reproduces all six quark masses, three charged lepton masses, and the CKM matrix. The same spurions mediate charged lepton flavour violation, neutral meson mixing, rare kaon decays, and induce electric dipole moments. We compare the reach on the compositeness scale $Λ_{\rm pre}$ across these observables in the numerical benchmark and find that the electron EDM and $K^0$-$\bar{K}^0$ mixing provide the strongest sensitivity, reaching $\mathcal{O}(10^4)$ TeV, the lower end of the range probed by proton decay, while $μ\to eγ$, $D^0$-$\bar{D}^0$ mixing, and $μ$-$e$ conversion give complementary reach at $10^2$-$10^3$ TeV. The projected electron EDM sensitivity extends this to $\mathcal{O}(10^6)$ TeV, beyond the reach of planned proton decay searches.

</details>


### [95] [Large-Field Vacuum Decay in General Multi-Scalar Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00216)
*Giorgio Busoni,Florian Goertz,Navneet Krishnan,Rickson Wielian*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a method for calculating tunneling rates in multi-scalar theories to assess vacuum stability, offering analytical expressions that are straightforward and applicable in various models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the potential finite lifetime of the universe due to false vacuum decay in multi-scalar theories, which may predict lower-energy vacua.

Method: The authors demonstrate that in the large-field regime, a one-dimensional radial bounce provides the leading contribution to tunneling rates, leading to the development of simple analytical expressions for calculating these rates using an effective quartic coupling.

Result: The study establishes that corrections to the tunneling rate are exponentially suppressed and allows for straightforward derivation of effective couplings in biquadratic scalar potentials. It also exemplifies its application in models like 2HDM+a and 3-3-1.

Conclusion: The results offer a robust framework for analyzing vacuum stability in various theoretical models, ensuring that lifetimes do not predict values much shorter than the universe's age.

Abstract: Many theories beyond the standard model exhibit multiple scalar particles. Such multi-scalar theories can in principle host lower-energy vacua, and thus predict that our universe has a finite lifetime due to false vacuum decay. This scenario cannot be ruled out a priori as even the standard model's electroweak vacuum has been shown to be metastable; however, for theoretical consistency, we still require that the model does not predict a lifetime much smaller than the age of the universe. The calculation of these tunneling rates at leading order for multi-scalar theories typically includes numerical approaches, or approximations which are frequently not analytically controlled. In this article we show that, in the large-field regime, a one-dimensional radial bounce always produces the exact dominant contribution to the leading order tunneling rate, with corrections being exponentially suppressed. This allows us to write simple analytical expressions to calculate the tunneling rate in multi-scalar theories, in terms of an effective quartic coupling $λ_\text{eff}$. For theories with biquadratic scalar potentials, we also derive straightforward analytical expressions for $λ_\text{eff}$ in terms of the original theory's couplings. Finally, we provide example applications of our results to study the vacuum stability of the 2HDM+a model and the 3-3-1 model.

</details>


### [96] [Determination of the $η$-$η^{\prime}$ Mixing Angle from $F_{η^{(\prime)}γ}(Q^2)$ within the Self-consistent Light-front Quark Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00407)
*Shuai Xu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Study of the $η$-$η^{	ext{'}}$ system using the light-front quark model reveals a mixing angle of $41.5^	ext{circ}$, consistent with LHCb, leading to predictions of various meson properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the nonperturbative properties of the $η$-$η^{	ext{'}}$ system and the implications of flavor mixing on decay properties.

Method: A self-consistent light-front quark model with quark flavor mixing, analyzed through a $χ^2$ fit of transition form factors using CLEO and BABAR data.

Result: The best fit mixing angle is $θ=41.5^	ext{circ}$ with calculated decay constants, LCDAs, and electromagnetic interaction radii consistent with recent experimental measurements.

Conclusion: TFFs provide significant constraints on the light-front description; future measurements are needed to confirm predictions for decay widths.

Abstract: We study the $η$-$η^{\prime}$ system in the self-consistent light-front quark model (LFQM) with the quark flavor mixing scheme, where the single mixing angle is constrained by the transition form factors (TFFs) $F_{η^{(\prime)}γ}(Q^2)$. A combined $χ^2$ scan of the CLEO and BABAR data yields the best fit value $θ=41.5^\circ$, in close agreement with the recent LHCb result $(41.6^{+1.0}_{-1.2})^\circ$. Using this mixing angle, we calculate the decay constants, light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs), Gegenbauer moments, $ξ$-moments, transverse momentum moments, electromagnetic interaction radii and two photon decay widths of the $η$ and $η^{\prime}$ mesons. The predicted $a_2^η$ and $\langleξ^2\rangle_η$ are consistent with the latest BABAR results, and the electromagnetic interaction radii also agreeing well with the A2 and BESIII measurements. These results demonstrate that TFFs data provide strong constraints on the light-front description for the nonperturbative properties of the $η$-$η^{\prime}$ system. Owing to the lack of direct experimental constraints on the real photon value $F_{η^{(\prime)}γ}(0)$, the predicted decay widths are somewhat lower than the experimental data, which can be further examined with future measurements.

</details>


### [97] [Matter-Induced CPT Violation and Earth-Density Stratification Effects in Long-Baseline Neutrino Oscillation Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00436)
*Tia Pandit,Bipin Singh Koranga,Vivek Kumar Nautiyal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the interrelated effects of matter-induced CPT violation and Earth density variation in long-baseline neutrino oscillation experiments, emphasizing their impact on CP-phase reconstruction and mass ordering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a comprehensive understanding of the correlated systematics of matter-induced CPT violation and Earth density stratification in neutrino oscillation experiments, which can affect the interpretation of CP violation and mass ordering.

Method: A unified $χ^2$ framework was developed to analyze the joint effects of CPT violation and density stratification, using exact three-flavour matrix exponentiation to compute the asymmetry $A_{μe}^{m CPT}$ for experiments like T2K, NO$ν$A, DUNE, and Hyper-Kamiokande.

Result: The asymmetry $A_{μe}^{m CPT}$ ranges from 0.022 to 0.180, with variations depending on mass ordering. Density uncertainties impact $A_{μe}^{m CPT}$ minimally, while density profile simplifications lead to significant biases in $δ_{m CP}$ reconstruction, particularly at larger baselines.

Conclusion: For accurate modeling of neutrino oscillation effects, it is essential to include correlated systematics of CPT violation and density variations, employing a joint statistical treatment.

Abstract: We present a unified analysis of two matter-potential systematics in long-baseline (LBL) neutrino oscillation experiments, treating matter-induced extrinsic CPT violation and Earth density stratification as correlated systematics within a single $χ^2$ framework. We study their joint impact on CP-phase reconstruction and mass-ordering dependence. Matter-induced extrinsic CPT violation produces a non-zero asymmetry $A_{μe}^{\rm CPT}=(P_{μe}-\bar P_{μe})/(P_{μe}+\bar P_{μe})$, computed using exact three-flavour matrix exponentiation for T2K, NO$ν$A, DUNE, and Hyper-Kamiokande. The asymmetry ranges from 0.022 to 0.180 at the respective peak energies and differs by up to $9\%$ between normal and inverted orderings. The surface $A_{μe}^{\rm CPT}(E,δ_{\rm CP})$ at DUNE reveals an interplay between extrinsic CPT and intrinsic CP violation in the high-$L/E$ regime requiring joint statistical treatment. We quantify the sensitivity of $A_{μe}^{\rm CPT}$ to density uncertainties $ρ\rightarrowρ(1+\varepsilon)$ with $\varepsilon=\pm1\%,\pm2\%,\pm5\%$, finding that a $\pm5\%$ uncertainty induces $|ΔA_{\rm CPT}|<0.008$ at DUNE. Replacing the PREM profile with a constant path-averaged density yields a $δ_{\rm CP}$ reconstruction bias below $0.3^\circ$ for $L\le5000$ km, increasing to $17.8^\circ$ at $7000$ km and $172.2^\circ$ at $12000$ km. Since both effects arise from the same matter Hamiltonian, they must be modelled jointly using a Poisson log-likelihood $χ^2$ with nuisance-parameter pulls.

</details>


### [98] [Kinetic backreaction cannot suppress axion quantum pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00472)
*Kaleb Anderson,Savvas M. Koushiappas*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that ultralight axions with non-canonical kinetic terms cannot enhance structure growth via oscillation backreactions, as they increase the Jeans length instead.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether ultralight axion dark matter can enhance structure growth by reducing the Jeans length through backreaction effects.

Method: Analytical techniques using adiabatic-invariant analysis and direct numerical integration are employed to explore the effects of oscillation-averaged backreaction on the coupling function of a non-canonical kinetic term.

Result: The study finds that for any smooth, positive coupling function, oscillation-averaged backreaction increases the coupling, thus reinforcing quantum pressure and enlarging the Jeans length with time.

Conclusion: Kinetically coupled dark matter cannot facilitate enhanced gravitational collapse or seeding of structure on scales smaller than the Jeans scale.

Abstract: Ultralight axions from string compactifications carry non-canonical kinetic terms whose normalization is set by a coupling function that depends on an accompanying modulus field. As dark matter, such an axion forms a cold condensate whose quantum pressure resists gravitational collapse below a Jeans scale fixed by that coupling. One might hope that backreaction from the condensate's oscillations could shrink this Jeans length and thereby enhance structure growth. We show that it cannot. For any smooth, positive coupling function, the oscillation-averaged backreaction always drives the coupling to larger values, strengthening the quantum pressure and growing the Jeans length with time. We establish this no-go result analytically through an adiabatic- invariant analysis and confirm it by direct numerical integration. Kinetically coupled dark matter therefore cannot self-generate the conditions for enhanced gravitational collapse or the seeding of structure on sub-Jeans scales.

</details>


### [99] [Nelson-Barr Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00498)
*Fuminobu Takahashi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The Nelson-Barr mechanism addresses the strong CP problem via spontaneous CP violation, proposing a model where a scalar field facilitates hilltop inflation without regenerating domain walls post-inflation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the strong CP problem and explore its implications in cosmology, particularly regarding inflation, reheating, and baryogenesis.

Method: Utilization of a CP-symmetric double-well potential for the Nelson-Barr scalar, facilitating inflation in one valley and avoiding domain wall regeneration post-inflation through energy considerations.

Result: The model predicts an elevated scalar spectral index compatible with CMB observations and establishes connections between the Nelson-Barr mechanism and visible-sector reheating, as well as potential leptogenesis when coupled with right-handed neutrinos.

Conclusion: This framework integrates the dynamics of the Nelson-Barr mechanism, addressing several cosmological challenges while preventing domain wall issues.

Abstract: The Nelson-Barr mechanism solves the strong CP problem through spontaneous CP violation, but the resulting CP-conjugate vacua generically lead to stable domain walls. We propose that the Nelson-Barr scalar itself drives hilltop inflation. A CP-symmetric double-well potential for its imaginary component forms two CP-breaking valleys, with inflation proceeding along one of them. Since the lowest CP-invariant ridge is generically much higher than the energy available after inflation, transitions between the two branches are energetically inaccessible, and the domain walls cannot be regenerated after inflation. A small CP-preserving linear deformation raises the scalar spectral index of quartic hilltop inflation and yields CMB-compatible parameter regions. The Nelson-Barr sector necessarily provides a portal to visible-sector reheating. When coupled to right-handed neutrinos, it can also transmit the spontaneous CP phase to the lepton sector and, in an extension that allows a higher CP-breaking scale, even realize nonthermal leptogenesis. This establishes a unified cosmological realization of the Nelson--Barr mechanism in which the same CP-breaking dynamics solves the strong CP problem, can generate quark and lepton CP violation, drives inflation and reheating, and enables baryogenesis, while simultaneously eliminating the associated domain-wall problem.

</details>


### [100] [Dipole-dipole scattering at high energy in the Pomeron field theory with Braun Hamiltonian and beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00506)
*Eugene Levin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper identifies the scattering matrix for dipole-dipole interactions at high saturation, revealing it behaves exponentially based on established models, contradicting previous approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the scattering matrix in the context of high-energy dipole-dipole interactions and its implications for theoretical frameworks like Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) amplitudes.

Method: The authors utilize a theoretical framework focused on Pomeron interactions at leading $1/N_c$ approximation, proving their results mathematically for both the dipole-dipole scattering matrix and the BK amplitude.

Result: The scattering matrix ${	ilde S}^d_d$ in the saturation region behaves as $ 	ext{exp}(-C^d_d z^2) 	imes ({	ilde S}_{BK})^4$, contrasting with 'rare' fluctuation and large Pomeron loop summation predictions.

Conclusion: Large Pomeron loops have a reliable effect in high-energy interactions, supported by insights on Hamiltonian structure, promoting further exploration in the energy region $Y 	ext{≤} 1/	ext{as}^4$.

Abstract: In this paper we find that the scattering matrix for dipole-dipole interaction in the saturation region has the form: ${\cal S}^d_d \xrightarrow{z \,\gg\,1} \exp\Lb - C^d_d z^2\Rb \propto \Lb {\cal S}_{BK}\Rb^4 = \exp\Lb - 4\,C_{BK} z^2\Rb$, where $z = \bas κY\,+\,\ln\Lb \frac{r^2}{r'^2}\Rb$ for interaction of a dipole $r$ at rapidity $Y$ with dipole $r'$ at rest. $S_{BK}$ is the S-matrix for the Balitsky-Kovchegov amplitude. All constants are determined in the text. The proof is given in the same theoretical framework for both cases (${\cal S}^d_d$ and ${\cal S}_{BK}$): the Pomeron interaction which takes into account the Pomeron vertices in the leading $1/N_c$ approximation ($N_c$ is the number of colours). This result is in striking contradiction with 'rare' fluctuation approach as well as with summing the large Pomeron loops. These lead to ${\cal S}^d_d \propto \sqrt{{\cal S}_{BK}}$ at large $z$. In the paper we collect arguments supporting the idea that the sum of large Pomeron loops can be trusted in a wide region of energy: $Y \leq 1/\as^4$. In addition we discuss the influence of the structure of the Hamiltonian on the asymptotic behaviour of the scattering amplitudes using the exactly solvable one dimensional model as our theoretical ground.

</details>


### [101] [Mass spectra of Tetraquark States from the Spinless Salpeter Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00553)
*Pranami Baishya,Bhaskar Jyoti Hazarika*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the mass spectrum of tetraquark states using a spinless Salpeter equation and Cornell potential, providing predictions for their masses in a diquark-antidiquark model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the mass spectra of heavy tetraquark states and improve the understanding of their internal structure and dynamics.

Method: The spinless Salpeter equation and Cornell potential are employed, using a variational approach to derive mass formulas and account for relativistic effects through first-order perturbation theory.

Result: The calculated tetraquark masses are sensitive to the strong coupling constant and align well with several experimentally observed and predicted tetraquark candidates.

Conclusion: The study provides theoretical mass predictions which enhance the understanding of heavy tetraquarks, confirming some previous experimental observations.

Abstract: The mass spectrum of tetraquark states is examined using the spinless Salpeter equation alongside the Cornell potential. Analytical wave functions are derived through a variational approach, resulting in a specific mass formula for the bound states. This study investigates the mass spectra of heavy tetraquark states within a constituent diquark-antidiquark model. We employ a spinless Salpeter equation framework, incorporating the Cornell potential to describe the interaction between the diquark and antidiquark constituents. To account for relativistic effects, we utilize first-order perturbation theory to include the leading-order kinetic energy corrections arising from the expansion of the relativistic kinetic energy operator. A variational approach is adopted to determine the ground state masses, where the optimal variational parameter $β$ is found by minimizing the system's energy and then for higher states. We systematically calculate the masses of several experimentally observed and predicted tetraquark candidates, across a range of strong coupling constant values. Our results demonstrate a sensitivity of the calculated tetraquark masses to, and the model provides theoretical mass predictions that are in reasonable agreement with experimental observations for these exotic hadronic states. This work contributes to a deeper understanding of the internal structure and dynamics of heavy tetraquarks.

</details>


### [102] [Pion-photon transition form factor with analytic coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00564)
*I. A. Zemlyakov,I. L. Chuev,A. V. Kotikov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study assesses the pion-photon transition form factor using analytic QCD, contrasting results from conventional and analytic perturbative QCD with experimental data, finding the latter aligns well with observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To evaluate the effectiveness of conventional vs. analytic perturbative QCD in describing pion-photon transition form factors, as indicated by experimental data.

Method: The authors employed analytic QCD to analyze the pion-photon transition form factor, comparing both conventional and analytic perturbative approaches incorporating a 'massive' twist-four contribution.

Result: While conventional perturbation theory fails to match experimental data, the analytic approach shows a strong agreement with the observed results.

Conclusion: The findings highlight the superiority of analytic perturbative QCD over conventional methods in accurately describing experimental observations related to the pion-photon transition form factor.

Abstract: We investigate the pion-photon transition form factor within the framework of analytic QCD. A comparison is performed between experimental data and perturbative QCD based on conventional and analytic versions of perturbation theory with the ``massive'' form of the twist-four contribution. We show that conventional perturbation theory fails to reproduce the data, while the analytic version demonstrates good agreement with experiment.

</details>


### [103] [Complex chromoelectric polarizability of a heavy-quarkonium resonance: pole definition and channel-complete pNRQCD matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00611)
*Arkadiy I. Syamtomov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper examines the chromoelectric polarizability of quarkonium states, comparing stable and unstable cases through various theoretical models and matching techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the polarizability and properties of quarkonium resonances, particularly how stable and unstable states behave under certain transitions.

Method: Utilizing pNRQCD matching and pole-residue definitions, the study analyzes the normalization of the complete two-field vertex and its implications on chromoelectric polarizability.

Result: The study derives a residue factor for a minimal model related to the Psi(3770) pole, showing that normalization effects can be significant but depend on specific input parameters.

Conclusion: Absolute polarizability measurements require additional data on quarkonium and channel responses to achieve independence from the model.

Abstract: For a stable compact quarkonium, chromoelectric polarizability is generated by two E1 transitions through virtual color-octet states. An unstable quarkonium is instead defined by an isolated complex pole. We define its polarizability by the quadratic displacement of that pole. Combining the standard pole-residue definition of resonance properties with pNRQCD matching shows that the complete two-field vertex is normalized by the energy derivative of the full inverse propagator. Its numerator contains octet E1-E1 propagation, the field dependence of decay channels, and local hard matching terms, without double counting. The stable-state result is recovered with its standard sign and color factor. A minimal single-threshold model calibrated to the Psi(3770) pole gives, within its specified field convention, the residue factor 0.800-0.336i. This illustrates a potentially sizable normalization effect but is not a model-independent pole observable. The absolute polarizability still requires quarkonium and channel response inputs.

</details>


### [104] [Scalaron-driven Dark Matter during Warm Inflation via UV Freeze--in](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00874)
*F. Millo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores Dark Matter production during warm Higgs--Starobinsky inflation using a UV freeze-in mechanism, highlighting a unique timing and characteristics of DM yield close to the end of inflation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how Dark Matter is produced in the context of warm Higgs--Starobinsky inflation and to differentiate its dynamics from other inflationary models.

Method: Utilizing the UV freeze-in mechanism with non-renormalizable interactions of mass dimensions 8 and 9 during strong dissipation regimes.

Result: The study finds significant DM production for two mass scales (1 MeV and 100 GeV) occurring near the end of inflation, leading to the current DM relic abundance.

Conclusion: The HS potential and scalaron dynamics enhance DM production efficiency, indicating the HS model's distinctiveness in the realm of inflationary cosmology.

Abstract: We investigate the production of Dark Matter (DM) within the warm Higgs--Starobinsky (HS) inflation. By adopting the ultraviolet (UV) freeze--in mechanism -- where the DM number density is initially negligible but is populated over time through non-renormalizable interactions of defined mass dimensions -- we find that the DM production within the HS potential has a characteristic timing. For both DM masses, $m_χ=1$ MeV and $m_χ=100$ GeV, the yield of DM occurs close to the termination of inflation and its transition to the radiation dominated (RD) era. The present--day DM relic abundance is generated for both DM masses with non-renormalizable operators of mass dimension $D=\{8,9\}$ within strong dissipation regimes. The obtained results suggest that the form of the HS potential and the associated scalaron dynamics favor UV freeze--in DM production as the scalaron approaches the minimum of the potential, where energy transfer to the thermal bath becomes particularly efficient. This distinctive transition behavior differentiates the HS model from other inflationary models and advances our understanding of DM production.

</details>


### [105] [Misconceptions About the Physics of the QCD Trace Anomaly from Renormalization in a Reducible Basis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01002)
*Chen Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses issues with the QCD trace anomaly, highlighting its UV independence and free anomaly characteristics, while addressing misunderstandings introduced by recent renormalization techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the implications of the QCD trace anomaly and correct misconceptions related to nucleon physics due to new renormalization methods.

Method: Analysis of features of the QCD trace anomaly, its independence from UV regularization, and examination of the effects of reducible-basis renormalization.

Result: The introduction of reducible-basis renormalization has led to misunderstandings about nucleon mass and internal forces, contradicting established characteristics of the QCD trace anomaly.

Conclusion: The findings suggest a need for renewed understanding of the QCD trace anomaly in nucleon physics, emphasizing traditional principles over new methods that obscure these concepts.

Abstract: The QCD trace anomaly is a well-established textbook result in quantum field theory with several prominent features: (1) it arises from the quantum breaking of scale symmetry at ultraviolet (UV) scales, yet is independent of the particular UV regulator used, whether lattice or dimensional regularization; (2) although it is nominally proportional to (${\cal O}(α_s)$), it is free of renormalization-scheme ambiguity; and (3) it is free of UV divergences and is therefore scale independent. Unfortunately, these important features have been undermined in the recently introduced reducible-basis renormalization, leading to misunderstandings of anomaly-related nucleon physics, including the origins of nucleon mass and internal forces.

</details>


### [106] [Thermal Evolution and Hydrodynamic Filtering of Pseudoscalar Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01100)
*Xiu-Fei Li,Hongxin Wang,Lei Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores how hydrodynamic effects during a first order phase transition can enhance the abundance of massive dark matter particles, analyzing both analytical and numerical methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the effects of hydrodynamics on the production and abundance of dark matter during a phase transition in a complex singlet extension of the Standard Model.

Method: The authors calculate the entropy normalized dark matter abundance $Y_χ$ in the deflagration regime using analytic and numerical approaches, particularly focusing on the influence of shock heating and hydrodynamic filtering.

Result: Shock heating significantly increases the dark matter abundance $Y_χ$ for various particle masses, with values varying from $4.3$ to $32$ for specific mass ranges, while the results show strong agreement between analytic and numerical methods, especially when hydrodynamic effects are considered.

Conclusion: Hydrodynamic reheating can enhance dark matter abundance even if it has already frozen out before the phase transition, indicating a significant interaction between dark matter and plasma dynamics during this process.

Abstract: Filtered dark matter provides a mechanism for producing massive dark matter particles during a first order phase transition. The resulting abundance can be modified by plasma hydrodynamics. We investigate this effect in a complex singlet extension of the Standard Model, focusing on the deflagration regime. The entropy normalized dark matter abundance $Y_χ$ is calculated using both analytic and numerical methods. As the dark matter mass increases, we identify three patterns of abundance evolution before nucleation, ranging from thermal equilibrium to thermal suppression and freeze-out. For $ξ_w=0.01$, we find that shock heating increases $Y_χ$ by factors of approximately $4.3$, $5.8$, and $32$ for $m_χ=1.78$, $2.30$, and $5.03~\mathrm{TeV}$, respectively. For the two lighter masses, the numerical and analytic results agree within $5\%$, and the difference is reduced to about $1\%$ or less when hydrodynamic effects are included. The heaviest case requires a numerical treatment because dark matter freeze-out before nucleation. Our results show that hydrodynamic reheating can enhance $Y_χ$ even when dark matter has already frozen out before the phase transition. In this regime, $Y_χ$ is determined by the dark matter abundance established before nucleation and hydrodynamic filtering at the bubble wall.

</details>


### [107] [Model analysis on the effectiveness of the HAL QCD method for hadron-hadron interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01225)
*Takayasu Sekihara,Kei Fujiwara*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the HAL QCD method's effectiveness in deriving hadron interactions from lattice QCD, demonstrating its ability to reproduce scattering properties and binding energies accurately.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the HAL QCD method's validity in deriving hadron-hadron interactions from lattice QCD simulation data.

Method: A formula is derived to connect HAL QCD quantities like R-correlators and local potentials to hadron-hadron scattering amplitudes using effective models.

Result: The HAL QCD local potentials accurately reproduce the phase shifts of hadron scatterings and their binding energies in scenarios of predominantly local interactions; modifications due to lattice discretization are minimal.

Conclusion: The HAL QCD method demonstrates consistency with original interactions, but singular behavior in the $ar{K} N$ transition amplitude necessitates further analysis.

Abstract: The HAL QCD method has been one of the powerful tools to extract hadron-hadron interactions directly from lattice QCD simulation data. In this paper, we aim at examining the effectiveness of the HAL QCD method by deriving a formula to calculate quantities in the HAL QCD method, such as the so-called R-correlators and HAL QCD local potentials, from the hadron-hadron scattering amplitudes within effective models. In this framework, we can judge whether the HAL QCD local potential, evaluated in the present formula, reproduces the properties of the original hadron-hadron interaction or not via the scattering amplitude, which is a solution of the Lippmann--Schwinger equation with the original hadron-hadron interaction as an input. In an analysis within a simple model of elastic scattering, we show that, when the original interaction is predominantly local, the HAL QCD local potentials quantitatively reproduce phase shifts of the hadron-hadron scatterings and correctly indicate the existence/absence of the bound state with its binding energy $\sim$ MeV. Lattice discretization of spacetime modifies the results only slightly. Furthermore, we consider the $\bar{K} N$ potential in a bare to $\bar{K} N$ transition amplitude, which shows singular behavior around the origin in the recent HAL QCD results of the lattice QCD simulation data, and discuss the cause of such singular behavior in the HAL QCD method through our model analysis of the $\bar{K} N$ scattering.

</details>


### [108] [A femtoscopic tale of two $C$-parities: the $Z_c(3900)$ and the isovector partner of the $X(3872)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01237)
*Pan-Pan Shi,Miguel Albaladejo,Feng-Kun Guo,Juan Nieves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores the exotic hadron states $Z_c(3900)$, $Z_{cs}(3985)$, and the $W_{c1}$, using femtoscopic correlation functions of charm meson-antimeson pairs to gain insights into their properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the properties of exotic hadrons and identify new states like the predicted $W_{c1}$, which challenge our understanding of particle physics.

Method: Femtoscopic correlation functions (CFs) of $D^{(ullet)0}D_{(s)}^{(ullet)-}$ pairs were analyzed within a coupled-channel framework, considering both $C$-odd and $C$-even contributions.

Result: The analysis revealed that the $C$-even admixture significantly enhances low-momentum CFs, indicating observable effects near thresholds, found at more than $2.5σ$.

Conclusion: These findings provide a novel method to investigate elusive exotic states in high-energy collisions, paving the way for potential discoveries at LHC.

Abstract: Understanding the nature of the exotic $Z_c(3900)$ and $Z_{cs}(3985)$ states, and searching for the predicted isovector partner $W_{c1}$ of the $X(3872)$, remain central challenges in exotic-hadron spectroscopy. We investigate the femtoscopic correlation functions (CFs) of the $D^{(\ast)0}D_{(s)}^{(\ast)-}$ systems. Since these charm-meson--antimeson pairs are not $G$-parity eigenstates, their CFs contain contributions from both the $C$-odd and $C$-even sectors, providing direct access to the dynamics underlying the $Z_c$, $Z_{cs}$, and the predicted isovector exotic $W_{c1}$. Within a heavy-quark-spin-symmetric coupled-channel framework, we show that the $C$-even admixture enhances the low-momentum CFs by more than $2.5σ$ in the vicinity of their thresholds. Free from Coulomb distortions and accessible in high-multiplicity $pp$ collisions at the LHC, these channels offer the first direct femtoscopic probe of the isovector $C$-even sector and of the elusive $W_{c1}$ state.

</details>


### [109] [Properties of the $D_{s0}^*(2317)^\pm$ in hot and dense nuclear matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01272)
*Tomona Kinugawa,Àngels Ramos,Laura Tolos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores the properties of the $D_{s0}^	imes(2317)$ mesons in nuclear matter, finding that density and temperature significantly alter their masses and widths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the behavior of exotic mesons like $D_{s0}^	imes(2317)$ in dense nuclear environments can provide insights into their internal structure and interactions.

Method: A coupled-channel molecular model utilizing next-to-leading-order heavy meson chiral perturbation theory was employed to study the in-medium modifications of the $D_{s0}^	imes(2317)$ mesons.

Result: As nuclear density increases, the $D_{s0}^	imes(2317)^+$ peak shifts lower and broadens, while temperature effects yield milder changes. In contrast, the $D_{s0}^	imes(2317)^-$ exhibits opposite behaviors under temperature variations, with its mass dropping and width increasing.

Conclusion: These findings highlight distinct medium dependencies for both $D_{s0}^	imes(2317)$ states, offering a pathway to better understand their internal structure.

Abstract: We investigate the properties of the $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^\pm$ in hot and dense nuclear matter using a coupled-channel molecular model built on next-to-leading-order heavy meson chiral perturbation theory. In-medium modifications to the $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^+$ stem from changes to the $DK$ channel within the coupled $DK$-$D_s η$ system. As nuclear density increases, the $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^+$ quasiparticle peak shifts toward lower energies and broadens, tracking the behavior of the $D$-meson spectral function. As for temperature effects, those are milder, with the thermal smearing of the Fermi surface and the melting of $Σ_c N^{-1}$ excitations in the $D$-meson spectral function shifting the $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^+$ peak back toward its free-space mass while narrowing it. Conversely, the behavior of the $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^-$ is governed by the $\bar D \bar K$ channel and its medium behavior is driven by the $\bar K$ spectral function. With increasing temperature, the $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^-$ also approaches its free-space mass, but its width broadens before saturating at high temperatures. Incorporating explicit medium dependencies into the interaction kernel, driven by density and/or temperature variations in the pion decay constant, further shifts the $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^+$ mass lower and narrows its width with temperature. As for $D_{s0}^\ast(2317)^-$, its mass also drops with temperature but its width increases. These contrasting medium behaviors offer a promising pathway to constrain the internal structure of these exotic states.

</details>


### [110] [Constraints on Light Self-Interacting Dark Matter from Plasmon-Enhanced Direct Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01305)
*Wen-Na Yang,Wu-Long Xu,Wenyu Wang,Ning Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study examines light self-interacting dark matter (SIDM) mediated by light particles, focusing on detecting it through silicon detectors fueled by high-energy cosmic rays, and sets exclusion limits based on existing data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address small-scale structure anomalies in galactic formations linked to light dark matter models using long-range self-interactions facilitated by light mediators.

Method: Investigate the sensitivity of silicon detectors to light SIDM using data from public SENSEI and DAMIC-M experiments, leveraging electronic collective excitations for detection.

Result: The study establishes 90% confidence level exclusion limits for light SIDM, covering some regions of the parameter space that align with galactic small-scale anomalies.

Conclusion: The findings enhance the understanding of SIDM and its potential interactions, contributing valuable constraints to ongoing searches for light dark matter.

Abstract: Models of light dark matter often invoke a light mediator to facilitate interactions with the Standard Model. If sufficiently light, this mediator can induce long-range self-interactions among dark matter particles, offering a compelling resolution to small-scale structure anomalies. However, direct detection of light self-interacting dark matter (SIDM) remains challenging for conventional detectors. In this work, we investigate the sensitivity of searches for light SIDM accelerated by high-energy cosmic rays in silicon detectors. Leveraging the electronic collective excitations, we derive 90\% C.L. exclusion limits using public SENSEI and DAMIC-M ionization data. Our constraints can cover a portion of the light SIDM parameter space favored by galactic small-scale anomalies.

</details>


### [111] [Impact of Higgs precision measurements at the LHC and FCC-ee on the spectrum of composite Higgs models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01353)
*Reza Asgharzadeh Jelodar,Kazem Bitaghsir Fadafan,Giacomo Cacciapaglia*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper discusses a minimal composite Higgs model based on $	ext{SU}(4)	o	ext{Sp}(4)$, analyzing the implications of Higgs coupling measurements for the vacuum alignment angle and singlet pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson mass. It utilizes both Bayesian and frequentist methods to derive constraints from current and projected data, revealing significant bounds on mass parameters for future collider experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the implications of Higgs couplings on the vacuum structure and mass of the singlet pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson in a minimal composite Higgs model.

Method: The study uses leading-order relations to connect Higgs coupling measurements to vacuum alignment angles, employing Bayesian analysis along with a frequentist $Δχ^2$ analysis to evaluate constraints on mass and alignment parameters.

Result: Current ATLAS data sets a conservative lower limit of about 440 GeV on the singlet mass at a 95% credibility level, with expectations from future collider experiments tightening this limit.

Conclusion: Future precision measurements of the Higgs boson will significantly enhance constraints on the minimal $	ext{SU}(4)/	ext{Sp}(4)$ model, making the singlet scalar a key focus for high-energy physics research.

Abstract: We investigate the minimal composite Higgs model based on the symmetry-breaking pattern $\mathrm{SU}(4)\rightarrow\mathrm{Sp}(4)$, where electroweak symmetry breaking is governed by the vacuum alignment angle $θ$. Through the leading-order relations $κ_V=\cosθ$ and $m_η=m_h/\sinθ$, precision measurements of Higgs couplings are translated into direct constraints on the vacuum structure and the singlet pseudo-Nambu--Goldstone boson mass. Using the current ATLAS Run-2 measurement of $κ_V$, we construct a Bayesian posterior for $θ$, including the Jacobian associated with the transformation from $κ_V$ to $θ$, and validate the results through an independent frequentist $Δχ^2$ analysis. The same framework is then applied to the projected sensitivities of the High-Luminosity LHC and FCC-ee. The current data imply a conservative lower bound of approximately $440~\mathrm{GeV}$ on the singlet mass at the $95\%$ credibility level, while the projected sensitivities improve this limit to about $600~\mathrm{GeV}$ at the HL-LHC and beyond $2~\mathrm{TeV}$ at FCC-ee. The close agreement between the Bayesian and frequentist determinations demonstrates the robustness of the extracted constraints. These results show that future Higgs precision measurements will probe the vacuum alignment of the minimal $\mathrm{SU}(4)/\mathrm{Sp}(4)$ composite Higgs model with unprecedented sensitivity, placing increasingly stringent constraints on the allowed parameter space and establishing the singlet scalar as a compelling target for upcoming collider programs.

</details>


### [112] [The CKM sector of the exceptional-Jordan programme: finite-Dirac mass moduli, two conditional angle estimates, and the weak-to-mass bridge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01445)
*Tejinder P. Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a model addressing quark masses and CKM matrix parameters through a novel algebraic framework, revealing specific numerical predictions and structural insights that extend current theoretical understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore quark masses and the CKM matrix, which are not determined by the Standard Model, using a new mathematical approach.

Method: Utilizes the exceptional-Jordan program involving square-root masses and Symmetric-Cube lifts to construct a finite Dirac operator that incorporates mass ratios and frames.

Result: Calculates specific CKM matrix elements with small deviations from experimental values and establishes relationships between mass ratios and CKM parameters, along with structural insights into the related algebraic framework.

Conclusion: The model provides a potential framework for understanding quark mass patterns and CKM matrix relationships, while leaving open questions regarding family selection and chiral projection.

Abstract: The Standard Model does not determine quark masses or the CKM matrix. In the exceptional-Jordan programme, square-root masses occupy short $\operatorname{Sym}^{3}(\mathbf{3})$ chains. Compressing the symmetric-cube lift onto occupied nodes gives a root-mass operator whose square yields the proposed mass ratios, packaging that spectrum and relative left frames in one finite Dirac operator without deriving the frames. Conditional on the transport, virtual-node-amplitude, real-$(2,3)$ and balanced-quadrature choices, the no-fit layer gives $|V_{us}|=0.2371$ ($5.3\%$ high) and $|V_{cb}|=0.0422$ ($0.8\%$ high) at $M_Z$. The relation $|V_{ub}|/|V_{cb}|=\sqrt{m_u/m_c}$ is a factor two low; one complex long edge is fitted. The two balanced orientations give branches $(\varepsilon,ω)=(0.002079,288.2^\circ)$ and $(0.005358,202.2^\circ)$, so the former $\varepsilon$ ratio is not robust and raw $ω$ is convention-dependent.
  For an adopted $F_1$--$F_2$ family embedding, we construct an exact Peirce-changing Albert lift in $\mathfrak f_4(\mathbb C)$, reproducing conjugate up/anti-down transport and $\varphi_{12}=-2χ$. For an adopted cyclic Majorana placement and real-linear projection, its completion has real $(e_4,e_3,e_6)$ support while the $e_1$ quadrature vanishes; $J_\ell=0$ and $δ^\ell_{CP}=0$ or $π$ are compatibility results in this class. The minimal radial-quartic cyclic truncation has equal-magnitude full-rank extrema or a flat direction; a mixed Albert cubic gives stable alignment only in a chosen three-edge subspace. Six diagonal mass links plus three directed Peirce links would form a connected one-cycle nine-link graph if the Yukawa block is linear in the projected bridge. This is a candidate Arkani-Hamed et al. texture skeleton, not a derivation: family-vacuum selection, chiral projection and right-frame locking remain open.

</details>


### [113] [What are the consequences of independent factorization and renormalization scales?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01489)
*T. C. Rogers,R. M. Whitehill*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper argues that in QCD factorization theorems, renormalization and factorization scales must be identical to maintain theoretical consistency and explores the implications for phenomenological applications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the importance of scale equality in QCD factorization theorems for ensuring renormalization group invariance and adherence to fundamental principles.

Method: Analysis of generalized pole subtraction schemes and collinear factorization theorems, particularly in the context of deep inelastic scattering.

Result: It is demonstrated that separate scales cannot coexist while preserving essential theoretical properties, affecting predictions and parameter extraction in QCD analyses.

Conclusion: The findings emphasize the need for consistency in scale choice, influencing the reliability of phenomenological calculations and connections to non-perturbative methods.

Abstract: It is common for separate factorization and renormalization scales to be discussed in connection with phenomenological applications of QCD factorization theorems. We observe that simultaneously preserving renormalization group invariance, Ward identities, and the basic sum rules in the definitions of parton densities forces these scales to be equal. The statement applies to generalized pole subtraction schemes that use dimensional regularization and to collinear factorization theorems for basic processes like deep inelastic scattering. We discuss implications for estimating the effects of scale sensitivity in phenomenological calculations, consistent extractions of Standard Model parameters alongside parton densities in global QCD analyses, and generally connecting phenomenologically extracted parton densities to first principles non-perturbative techniques like lattice QCD.

</details>

Sehgal,S. U. Shivashankara,G. Sigl,K. Simkova,F. Simon,R. Šmída,S. Soares Sippert,P. Sommers,S. Stanič,J. Stasielak,P. Stassi,S. Strähnz,M. Straub,T. Suomijärvi,A. D. Supanitsky,Z. Svozilikova,Z. Szadkowski,F. Tairli,A. Tapia,C. Taricco,C. Timmermans,O. Tkachenko,P. Tobiska,C. J. Todero Peixoto,B. Tomé,A. Travaini,P. Travnicek,C. Trimarelli,M. Tueros,M. Unger,R. Uzeiroska-Geyik,L. Vaclavek,M. Vacula,I. Vaiman,J. F. Valdés Galicia,L. Valore,P. van Dillen,E. Varela,V. Vašíčková,A. Vásquez-Ramírez,D. Veberič,I. D. Vergara Quispe,S. Verpoest,V. Verzi,J. Vicha,S. Vorobiov,J. B. Vuta,A. A. Watson,A. Weindl,M. Weitz,L. Wiencke,H. Wilczyński,B. Wundheiler,B. Yue,A. Yushkov,E. Zas,D. Zavrtanik,M. Zavrtanik*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study analyzes the Pierre Auger Observatory's potential to detect beyond-Standard Model physics through magnetic-moment-induced transitions between active and sterile neutrinos, impacting detection rates of ultrahigh energy neutrinos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how magnetic-moment interactions between neutrinos could affect their behavior and detection rates, particularly in the context of sterile neutrinos, which have not been fully characterized.

Method: The authors estimate the effects of dipole portal interactions on neutrino detection channels by analyzing both down-going and Earth-skimming neutrino events at ultrahigh energies, and set constraints based on the non-observation of UHE neutrinos.

Result: The research provides new constraints on the transition magnetic moments of sterile neutrinos, particularly for masses ranging from 1 TeV to 100 TeV, indicating broader limits compared to previous measurements.

Conclusion: The study extends the parameter space of sterile neutrino interactions, offering an enhanced understanding of potential physics beyond the Standard Model as inferred from UHE neutrino observations.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of the Pierre Auger Observatory to physics beyond the Standard Model arising from magnetic-moment-induced transitions between active and heavy sterile neutrinos. Such dipole portal interactions can enhance neutrino-nucleon cross sections above a kinematic threshold set by the sterile neutrino mass, leading to observable modifications of neutrino detection rates at ultrahigh energies (UHE). We estimate the impact of these interactions on both down-going and Earth-skimming neutrino detection channels, the contrasting responses of which enable discrimination between an enhanced neutrino flux and a modified interaction cross section. Using the non-observation of UHE neutrino candidates, we derive neutrino-flux-dependent constraints with 90% confidence-level on the transition magnetic moment for sterile neutrino masses in the range 1 TeV-100 TeV. Under the assumed flux scenarios, the resulting flavor-independent limits extend existing bounds into previously unexplored parameter space.

</details>


### [115] [Magnetic field modifications to the pion-quark vertex in the Linear Sigma Model with quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01701)
*Alejandro Ayala,Flávia Fialho,Adrián Lara,Ana Mizher,Javier Rendón*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes how a constant magnetic field modifies the quark-neutral pion vertex using the Linear Sigma Model, revealing changes at both tree-level and one-loop level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding vertex modifications in particle physics under magnetic fields can provide insights into QCD and hadron dynamics.

Method: The study employs the Linear Sigma Model and Ritus wave functions for calculation in configuration space, supplemented by one-loop modifications using the Schwinger proper-time method.

Result: Vertex modifications are computed for various Landau levels, demonstrating consistency across approaches with identical results for the lowest Landau level.

Conclusion: The findings enhance understanding of quark interactions in magnetic fields, confirming the stability of results across different computational methods.

Abstract: We compute the modification to the quark-neutral pion vertex, induced by a constant and uniform magnetic field, using the Linear Sigma Model with quarks as a low energy effective theory of QCD. The vertex is modified even at tree-level since, due to the loss of translational invariance, the calculation should be carried out in configuration space, with the quark described by Ritus wave functions instead of plane waves. We also compute the one-loop vertex modification. These modifications are found for arbitrary Landau levels occupied by the quark. We illustrate the result for the case where the quark occupies the lowest Landau level. To check the result, we also compute the one-loop vertex modification using the Schwinger proper-time method with the quark occupying the lowest Landau level and find the same result as in the case where the Ritus formalism is used.

</details>


### [116] [Jet-by-jet energy correlators as stochastic probes of the parton-to-hadron transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01764)
*Jingyu Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study investigates the distribution and covariance of angular weights in energy-energy correlators across jets, revealing non-Gaussian structures and discrepancies between parton and hadron levels in different event generators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analyze how energy-energy correlators fluctuate in specific angular regions from jet to jet, beyond conventional ensemble averages.

Method: By studying binned two-point correlators in a high-statistics Pythia sample and examining covariance traces, while also comparing parton- and hadron-level samples across different generators.

Result: Observations include non-zero cross-shell structures in covariance, a significant percentage of covariance retained after regression, and differing characteristics between parton and hadron levels under various configurations.

Conclusion: Jet-by-jet EEC covariance reveals insights into the parton-to-hadron transition not visible in the mean spectrum, highlighting differences between event generators.

Abstract: Energy-energy correlators are usually reported as ensemble averages and therefore do not specify how different angular regions fluctuate together from jet to jet. We retain the binned two-point correlator for each jet and study the distribution and covariance of its angular-shell weights. In a high-statistics Pythia sample, the shell variables are sparse and strongly non-Gaussian, and their covariance contains nonzero cross-shell structure spread over many modes. Regressing each shell on five jet-level summaries leaves 84-92% of the covariance trace, while shell-wise permutation and constituent-angle shuffling do not reproduce the observed off-diagonal correlations. We then compare separately generated parton- and hadron-level samples in Pythia and Herwig. Across 24 generator-radius-momentum configurations, the hadron-level covariance trace is smaller and the average neighboring-shell correlation over 0.30 <= r/R < 1 is larger. The size and angular dependence of the change differ between the generators, and a characteristic-position analysis does not identify a common scale. Jet-by-jet EEC covariance thus provides information on the parton-to-hadron transition that is absent from the mean spectrum.

</details>


### [117] [Odderon exchange in high-energy $K^0_S$ regeneration at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01768)
*P. Filip,M. Taševský,V. A. Khoze,R. Pasechnik,M. G. Ryskin,B. G. Zakharov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The study investigates detecting the Odderon through high-energy neutral-kaon regeneration in LHC conditions, focusing on K0L to K0S conversions and regeneration processes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the potential of detecting Odderon exchange using high-energy neutral-kaon regeneration, particularly in the context of LHC experiments.

Method: Analyzes both coherent-forward regeneration with various regenerator materials and non-forward regeneration at lower energies to estimate the influences of different contributions to the amplitude.

Result: Odderon-induced regeneration can create measurable distortions in decay distributions, but constraints such as primary K0S survival and competing processes limit clear detection.

Conclusion: The study outlines the challenges and strategies for accurately identifying Odderon signals in high-energy neutral-kaon regenerative processes.

Abstract: We revisit the possibility of detecting the Odderon exchange through high-energy neutral-kaon regeneration, focusing on the in-matter $K^0_L\to K^0_S$ conversion of $(\sim\!0.2-2)$~TeV $K^0_L$ mesons originating from $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$~TeV, and on the practical constraints of realizing such a measurement in the very-forward region of an LHC interaction point. The analysis has two complementary parts. First, we reproduce the original coherent-forward-regeneration estimates for a liquid-hydrogen regenerator and extend them to realistic C, Cu and Pb regenerators of an LHC-compatible geometry, including neutral-kaon attenuation. We show that an Odderon-induced regeneration phase produces a measurable distortion of the $K^0\toπ^0π^0$ decay-vertex distribution at TeV kaon energies, but that the survival of primary $K^0_S$ mesons from the interaction point imposes severe baseline requirements, while a competing electromagnetic $C=-1$ (photon-exchange) amplitude limits the interpretation of the coherent mode as a clean Odderon measurement. Second, we examine non-forward (diffractive) regeneration at lower kaon energies of $0.2-0.8$~TeV, where the primary-$K^0_S$ contamination is naturally suppressed, and estimate the competing Odderon, Pomeron--Odderon-cut, $ω$-Reggeon and photon-exchange contributions to the regeneration amplitude. We identify neutron-induced neutral-only strangeness production and inelastic Regge backgrounds as the dominant limitations, quantify the background suppression required for an observable Odderon signal, and formulate the ingredients of an active double-regenerator subtraction strategy.

</details>


### [118] [Gluon condensate effects on heavy quarkonium spectral functions and thermal dissociation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01801)
*Fei Wang,Zi-qiang Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This study explores how gluon condensate affects thermal spectral functions and the dissociation of heavy vector mesons in a quark-gluon plasma, revealing that it stabilizes these mesons against thermal fluctuations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the impact of gluon condensate on heavy quarkonia in a quark-gluon plasma, particularly their thermal spectral properties and stability against dissociation.

Method: An improved soft-wall AdS/QCD model is used, extended to finite temperature with a dilaton black hole geometry accounting for gluon condensate's backreaction, followed by numerical analysis of spectral resonance peaks.

Result: The study finds that increased temperature broadens and suppresses spectral peaks, indicating in-medium dissociation, while a stronger gluon condensate reduces this broadening and enhances spectral weight, indicating stability of heavy mesons.

Conclusion: The gluon condensate plays a significant role in stabilizing heavy quarkonia in the quark-gluon plasma, contradicting thermal dissociation effects, aligning with existing literature.

Abstract: We investigate how the gluon condensate modifies the thermal spectral functions and melting patterns of heavy vector mesons, specifically charmonium and bottomonium, using an improved soft-wall AdS/QCD model. The framework is extended to finite temperature via a dilaton black hole geometry that consistently incorporates the backreaction from the gluon condensate. We numerically track the evolution of spectral resonance peaks as functions of both temperature and gluon condensate strength. Our calculations reveal that increasing temperature systematically broadens and suppresses spectral peaks, signaling in-medium dissociation. In contrast, a stronger gluon condensate considerably mitigates peak broadening and enhances the spectral weight of both ground and excited states. This behavior indicates that the gluon condensate hinders thermal dissociation, thereby stabilizing heavy quarkonia within the quark-gluon plasma. These findings are consistent with existing studies and provide new holographic evidence for the stabilising role of the gluon condensate from the perspective of thermal spectral functions.

</details>


### [119] [Measuring the trilinear Higgs self-coupling in Higgs boson pair production at multi-TeV muon colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01888)
*Kingman Cheung,Jae Sik Lee,Soojin Lee,Chen Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates multi-TeV muon colliders' ability to measure the trilinear Higgs self-coupling modifier $κ_3$ through Higgs boson pair production, achieving tighter bounds than projected for HL-LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Measuring the trilinear Higgs self-coupling is essential for understanding the Higgs potential and advancing collider physics.

Method: The study analyzes Higgs boson pair production via vector boson fusion, employing advanced techniques like a jet-to-Higgs pairing network and topological data analysis, using data from different energy regions.

Result: At 3 TeV, they achieve bounds of $0.80<κ_3<1.29$ and at 10 TeV, $0.96<κ_3<1.05$, indicating significant sensitivity for the measurement of the coupling.

Conclusion: The results show that future multi-TeV muon colliders can provide measurements of the Higgs self-coupling that are far more precise than those anticipated for HL-LHC.

Abstract: The trilinear Higgs self-coupling determines the shape of the Higgs potential, and its measurement is a central goal of future colliders. We assess the sensitivity of multi-TeV muon colliders to the coupling modifier $κ_3$ in Higgs boson pair production via vector boson fusion, using the $b\bar{b}b\bar{b}$ final state at $\sqrt{s}=3$ TeV with $1$ ab$^{-1}$ and at $10$ TeV with $10$ ab$^{-1}$. Events are analyzed in two complementary regions, a resolved region with four jets and a boosted region with two large-radius jets. To extract the signal from backgrounds a few orders of magnitude larger, we combine a supervised jet-to-Higgs pairing network based on the SPANet approach, topological data analysis of the event energy flow, and two dedicated classifiers, $D_{\rm HH}$ for the signal-to-background separation and $D_{κ_3}$ for the $κ_3$ shape information. The coupling is extracted from a two-dimensional likelihood fit to the distribution of the two classifier outputs. Combining the two regions, we obtain $0.80<κ_3<1.29$ at $3$ TeV and $0.96<κ_3<1.05$ at $10$ TeV at $68\%$ confidence level. The $10$ TeV determination reaches the few-percent level in this statistics-limited projection, which surpasses by a large amount the precision projected for the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [120] [Thermal Leptogenesis in the BNT Model of Neutrino Mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01890)
*P. S. Bhupal Dev,Srubabati Goswami,Debashis Pachhar,Drona Vatsyayan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The Babu-Nandi-Tavartkiladze (BNT) model employs a scalar quadruplet and vector-like fermions to generate neutrino masses and explore thermal leptogenesis, showing a connection between TeV-scale new physics and successful leptogenesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to robustly understand the generation of neutrino masses and establish a framework for thermal leptogenesis within the BNT model, which integrates vector-like fermions and could be experimentally explored.

Method: Neutrino masses are generated through effective operators of dimensions 5 and 7 at different levels, while the viability of thermal leptogenesis is evaluated based on fermion triplet mass scales, particularly through analyzing the influence of vector-like fermions.

Result: The model can successfully yield neutrino masses below the sub-eV scale and achieve thermal leptogenesis, with the mass of vector-like fermions identified as crucial for the model's viability, particularly showing thresholds of M_Σ ≈ 10^7 GeV and lower in quasi-degenerate cases.

Conclusion: The BNT model effectively bridges the gap between high-energy physics and observable particle phenomenology, allowing for both sub-eV neutrino mass generation and experimentally testable leptogenesis at colliders.

Abstract: We investigate neutrino mass and thermal leptogenesis in the Babu-Nandi-Tavartkiladze (BNT) model featuring a scalar quadruplet ($Φ$) and a pair of vector-like fermion triplets ($Σ$). In this framework, neutrino masses are generated via an effective dimension-7 operator $LLHH(H^{\dagger}H)/Λ^3$ at the tree level and via the dimension-5 operator $LLHH/Λ$ at the one-loop level. It naturally accommodates sub-eV neutrino masses even if the new physics scale $Λ$ is $\mathcal{O}(\rm TeV)$, thus making the model a compelling target for experimental searches. We explore the viability of thermal leptogenesis in this model, which is distinct from the canonical seesaw-based leptogenesis due to the presence of vector-like fermions. We find that leptogenesis is viable for $M_Σ\gtrsim 10^{7}$ GeV for a hierarchical spectrum of fermion triplets. However, in the quasi-degenerate regime, resonant enhancement of the $CP$ asymmetry lowers this scale down to $\mathcal{O}({\rm TeV})$, reconciling successful leptogenesis with the originally motivated TeV-scale phenomenology and testability of the model at colliders.

</details>


### [121] [A quality-coupling relation in chiral $U(1)_{B-L}$ axion model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01897)
*Yu-Cheng Qiu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper discusses how a gauged chiral $U(1)_{B-L}$ symmetry can protect the quality of QCD axions, revealing implications for their coupling to Standard Model fermions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore mechanisms that enhance the stability and testability of QCD axions through gauge symmetries.

Method: The authors analyze spontaneous symmetry breaking of $U(1)_{B-L}$ through complex scalars, resulting in one degree of freedom being absorbed by the gauge boson and another becoming the QCD axion.

Result: The findings demonstrate that a high quality of axion leads to a significant anomalous coupling with the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, alongside a 'quality floor' for axion-fermion interactions.

Conclusion: The study extends the parameter space of QCD axions, making them more testable and establishing a framework for understanding their interactions in relation to gauge symmetries.

Abstract: The quality of the QCD axion can be protected by a gauge symmetry. This work considers a gauged chiral $U(1)_{B-L}$. The vacuum expectation values of two complex scalars break $U(1)_{B-L}$. One linear combination of their two phase degrees of freedom is eaten by the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson, while the other becomes the QCD axion. Since the PQ symmetry is accidental, there is no ambiguity in the PQ charge assignment. High axion quality implies a large anomalous coupling between the axion and the $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge boson (or gauged Majoron). A `quality floor', namely a minimal coupling determined by the axion quality, arises for the coupling between the axion and SM fermions, extending the parameter space of QCD axions toward improved testability.

</details>


### [122] [The $(2+1)$-dimensional Gross-Neveu-Yukawa model at finite temperature, density, and magnetic field within the Functional Renormalization Group](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02280)
*Justin L. P. Mauldin,Dirk H. Rischke*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores the phase diagram of the (2+1)-dimensional GNY model under various conditions using advanced techniques, revealing complex behaviors in the chiral condensate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the effects of finite temperature, density, and magnetic fields on the GNY model beyond mean-field theory.

Method: Utilizes the Functional Renormalization Group method in the local potential approximation, enhanced by a hydrodynamical algorithm to solve the flow equation for the effective potential.

Result: The chiral condensate shows complex behaviors, including first-order phase transitions and oscillations, with a critical endpoint that shifts with magnetic field strength.

Conclusion: The findings highlight the intricate relationship between magnetic fields and phase transitions in the system, informing future studies on similar models.

Abstract: We investigate the phase diagram of the (2+1)-dimensional Gross-Neveu-Yukawa (GNY) model at finite temperature, density, and magnetic field beyond mean-field, using the Functional Renormalization Group (FRG) in the local potential approximation. Large magnetic fields result in magnetic catalysis, a dimensional reduction of the system, and enhancement of chiral symmetry breaking. We employ a hydrodynamical algorithm to solve the FRG flow equation for the effective potential, which allows for go further into the infrared region than with previously used methods. We find that the chiral condensate exhibits non-trivial behavior in various regions of the phase diagram: several first-order phase transitions and de Haas -- van Alphen oscillations at small magnetic field and large chemical potential, as well as a critical endpoint which shifts to higher temperature with increasing magnetic field.

</details>


### [123] [FCC precision requests: challenges for Monte Carlos and phenomenology tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02476)
*Z. Was*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper outlines three levels of ambiguities in accelerator experiments, emphasizing the necessity of Monte Carlo simulations for precision physics. It highlights significant contributions from various scholars and the evolution of methodologies and software tools in the field over many years.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the complexities of precision physics in accelerator experiments and the role of computational tools in mitigating ambiguities.

Method: Analysis of existing methodologies and software projects was conducted, focusing on their theoretical underpinnings and collaborative efforts in precision physics.

Result: It identifies three levels of ambiguity and underscores notable contributions from researchers, while highlighting areas that remain inadequately documented.

Conclusion: Extensive development in this area has been nonlinear, leading to simplifications and biases, as well as a need for a more comprehensive literature review.

Abstract: One can define three levels of ambiguities for the accelerator experiments: worse than 0.3%, around 0.3% and better than 0.3%. I will address issues important, for the last case, when Monte Carlo programs that simultaneously account for theoretical and experimental effects are necessary. To complete efforts, simultaneous effort in many specialities over years was necessary. The monumental projects of Bryan Lynn, Robin Stuart, Dima Bardin, Wolfgang Hollik and contributions of S. Jadach in the domain of precision physics need to be mentioned, they provide theoretical foundations. Incomplete lists of methodology domains and projects, usually left into references, appendices, and private notes include: (i) Phase space--> symmetries (ii) matrix element preparation --> factorizations (iii) program and development process design (iv) testing strategies (v) user interaction (vi) software tools (vii) partners and competitors.
  Usually we were publishing our own projects, let me mention incomplete lists of methodology domains and projects, which were usually left aside into references, appendices, and private notes: The work started on the basis of previous efforts which can be listed following names of the programs: (i) FOWL, (ii) GENRAP, (iii) Mustraal, (iv) Koralb, (v) Lesko, (vi) Tauola, (vii) KoralZ, (viii) Lumlog, (ix) Oldbab, (x) Bhlumi, (xi) Bhwide and (xii) KKMC. I will focus on some of these points. Others, hopefully, are covered in other talks. In particular I do not need to cover exponentiation and some issues of factorization; see contributions to the proceedings by B.F.L. Ward and A. Tapadar. Developments took years and did not follow a straight line; that is why there are simplifications, biases. Also a review of literature could not be completed.

</details>


### [124] [Two-loop amplitude for $t\bar{t}W$ production at hadron colliders in the leading colour approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02527)
*Mattia Pozzoli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents the first exact calculation of the leading-colour two-loop QCD amplitude for top-anti-top and W boson production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an exact calculation of relevant QCD amplitudes in high-energy physics, addressing the intricate structures involved.

Method: Utilizes strategies to manage analytic complexity, employing elliptic functions and differential equations for evaluation.

Result: The final result is expressed through special functions and rational coefficients derived using finite field techniques.

Conclusion: The work enhances the understanding of QCD processes in particle physics, establishing new computational methodologies.

Abstract: In this contribution I present the first exact calculation of the leading-colour two-loop QCD amplitude for the associated production of a top-anti-top pair and a W boson. I discuss strategies to address the complexity of the computation, which involves complicated analytic structures, such as nested square roots, elliptic functions, and expressions with a high degree of algebraic complexity. The final result is expressed in terms of a set of special functions, which are evaluated using the method of differential equations, and rational coefficients, evaluated via finite field techniques.

</details>


<div id='astro-ph.HE'></div>

# astro-ph.HE [[Back]](#toc)

### [125] [Polarization Angle Swings in Blazars Detected in the Millimeter-wave with the South Pole Telescope](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00302)
*A. Simpson,E. Anderes,A. J. Anderson,B. Ansarinejad,M. Archipley,L. Balkenhol,D. R. Barron,P. S. Barry,K. Benabed,A. N. Bender,B. A. Benson,F. Bianchini,L. E. Bleem,S. Bocquet,F. R. Bouchet,E. Camphuis,M. G. Campitiello,J. E. Carlstrom,J. Carron,C. L. Chang,P. M. Chichura,A. Chokshi,T. -L. Chou,A. Coerver,T. M. Crawford,C. Daley,T. de Haan,K. R. Dibert,M. A. Dobbs,M. Doohan,D. Dutcher,C. Feng,K. R. Ferguson,N. C. Ferree,K. Fichman,A. Foster,S. Galli,A. E. Gambrel,A. K. Gao,F. Ge,F. Guidi,S. Guns,N. W. Halverson,A. D. Hincks,E. Hivon,G. P. Holder,W. L. Holzapfel,J. C. Hood,A. Hryciuk,N. Huang,E. Järvelä,T. Jhaveri,F. Kéruzoré,A. R. Khalife,L. Knox,K. Kornoelje,C. -L. Kuo,K. Levy,Y. Li,A. E. Lowitz,C. Lu,G. P. Lynch,X. Ma,T. J. Maccarone,G. Madejski,A. S. Maniyar,E. S. Martsen,F. Menanteau,M. Millea,J. Montgomery,Y. Nakato,T. Natoli,A. Ouellette,Z. Pan,K. A. Phadke,A. W. Pollak,K. Prabhu,W. Quan,M. Rahimi,A. Rahlin,C. L. Reichardt,M. Rouble,J. E. Ruhl,A. C. Silva Oliveira,J. A. Sobrin,A. A. Stark,C. Tandoi,C. Trendafilova,J. D. Vieira,A. G. Vieregg,A. Vitrier,Y. Wan,N. Whitehorn,W. L. K. Wu,M. R. Young,J. A. Zebrowski*

Main category: astro-ph.HE

TL;DR: This paper investigates electric vector position angle (EVPA) swings in millimeter-wave emissions of blazars, finding a correlation with gamma-ray flares in some cases, but concludes that these swings may not be primarily responsible for the observed flares.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically explore EVPA swings in millimeter-wave emissions of blazars and their potential connections to gamma-ray flares, as previous studies suggested a relationship at optical wavelengths.

Method: Analyzed five years of observations from the South Pole Telescope, identifying bright sources with sufficient polarization signal for EVPA measurement and correlation with Fermi LAT gamma-ray data.

Result: Detected EVPA swings in four gamma-ray-active blazars, with swing characteristics differing from those observed at optical wavelengths; failed simulations indicated current models are inadequate for explaining observed swings.

Conclusion: While some EVPA swings cluster near gamma-ray flares, overall statistical analysis suggests that these associations might occur by chance rather than indicating a causal relationship.

Abstract: We present the first systematic search for electric vector position angle (EVPA) swings in the millimeter-wave (mm-wave) emission of blazars, using five years of observations from the South Pole Telescope SPT-3G camera at 95 and 150 GHz, and investigate their connection to gamma-ray flares. Of the 168 bright sources in the ~1500 square degrees SPT-3G Main Field, eight have sufficient polarization signal-to-noise for reliable EVPA measurement, four of which have continuous Fermi LAT gamma-ray detections. We detect EVPA swings in all four gamma-ray-active blazars and in none of the remaining four, consistent with the established connection between EVPA swings and high-energy emission seen at optical wavelengths. The observed swing amplitudes and rotation rates are smaller than those found in optical studies, consistent with mm-wave EVPA variability being slower than at shorter wavelengths. Random walk simulations of the polarization angle using a multi-cell model fail to reproduce both the number, amplitude, and duration of the observed swings, suggesting that this mechanism is insufficient to explain the observed swing population. Analysis of EVPA variability on one-week timescales is consistent with the anti-correlation between polarization degree and EVPA rotation rate previously observed at optical wavelengths. Of the 14 detected swings, eight are within 30 days of a gamma-ray flare. While many individual swing-flare associations are found to have a very low probability of happening by chance, the full ensembles of 95 and 150 GHz swing time lags with respect to gamma-ray flares are found to be consistent with random coincidence.

</details>


### [126] [Fast Population Leakage in Astronomical Masers: Maser Amplification and Transient Superradiance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00332)
*Vahid Anari,Toktam Rashidi,Fereshteh Rajabi*

Main category: astro-ph.HE
